<script>
  // Restore the saved color scheme preference, or
  // enact the browser preference if "automatic", 
  // without expecting DOM load to have completed.
  //
  // Also restore any saved readingmode and ToC display preferences.
  function initializeReadingPreferences() {
    let saved_theme = localStorage.getItem("ar5iv_theme") || "automatic";
    if (saved_theme === "automatic") {
      if (window.matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: dark)").matches) {
        saved_theme = "dark";
      }
    }
    if (saved_theme == "dark") {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-theme", "dark");
    } else {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-theme", "light");
    }

    const tocDisplay = localStorage.getItem('arxiv_html_paper_toc_display');
    if (tocDisplay) {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-toc-display", tocDisplay);
    }
    const readingMode = localStorage.getItem('arxiv_html_paper_reading_mode');
    if (readingMode) {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-reading-mode", readingMode);
    }
  }
  // Run as soon as JS starts, to minimize repainting
  initializeReadingPreferences();
</script>

  .arxiv-html-header { display: contents; }
  html[data-reading-mode="enabled"] :is(.arxiv-html-site-chrome, .arxiv-html-header, .ds-site-footer) { display: none; }
  /* The paper controls' icons are blocks, so they sit centred (the pinned JS shows the theme
     icon by making its span a block). */
  .arxiv-html-site-chrome .ds-site-header-tools svg { display: block; width: 18px; height: 18px; flex-shrink: 0; fill: currentColor; }
  /* ar5iv dims every image in dark mode and puts it on a white wash, for paper figures;
     arXiv's own render as they are (the logo on the bar, the funder logos on their white
     chips). Important: ar5iv's dark rules are unlayered, as specific as
     :root:not([data-theme]) img:not(.ltx_no_dark_filter). */
  :is(.ds-site-header, .ds-site-footer) img { filter: none !important; }
  .ds-site-header img { background-color: transparent !important; }

  /* Wide (the theme's 1280px): the theme lays the body out as a grid whose full-width top rows
     were the band's and its sticky reader bar's. The band takes the first and scrolls away; the
     header, the paper's bar (no site navigation, the paper's controls), takes the second and
     stays in view, the one sticky bar, with the anchor clearance that requires (DESIGN-POLICIES).
     The controls show their names. */
  @media (min-width: 1280px) {
    html { scroll-padding-top: 80px; }
    .arxiv-html-site-chrome { display: contents; }
    .arxiv-html-site-chrome > #ds-announcement { grid-area: announce; }
    .arxiv-html-site-chrome > .ds-site-header { grid-area: header; position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 3; }
    .arxiv-html-site-chrome .tool-icon { display: none; }
  }
  /* Narrow: the header scrolls away, and the controls show icons (names kept for screen
     readers; Why HTML? goes). The theme's contents panel is fixed below where its old sticky
     bar was, and hidden only once the reader has closed it, so until then it is an invisible box
     over the header and the paper: it shows only while open, under the chrome, in its place in
     the page. The paper's footer (build logs, reporting instructions) keeps the text's side
     inset; the theme gives it none, so it met the screen edges. */
  @media (max-width: 1279px) {
    .arxiv-html-site-chrome .tool-wide { display: none; }
    .arxiv-html-site-chrome .tool-label { position: absolute; width: 1px; height: 1px; overflow: hidden; clip: rect(0 0 0 0); white-space: nowrap; }
    html:not([data-toc-display="block"]) .ltx_page_navbar { display: none; }
    .ltx_page_navbar { position: absolute; top: auto; }
    .arxiv-html-footer { padding-inline: 1rem; }
  }

<div class="ltx_page_main">

<article class="ltx_document">
<h1 class="ltx_title ltx_title_document">Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under 
<br class="ltx_break">Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families</h1>
<div class="ltx_authors">
<span class="ltx_creator ltx_role_author">
<span class="ltx_personname">Zhaohui Wang
</span></span>
</div>

<div id="abstract1" class="ltx_abstract">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_title_abstract">Abstract</h6>
<p class="ltx_p">Modern LLMs are deployed as families of post-trained variants (base, instruct, chat, code) derived from a shared set of pre-trained weights. We present an empirical study of how post-training transforms weight-space geometry, covering eight configurations across four architecture families (Qwen2.5, Llama-3.1/3.2, Mistral, Gemma-2). We identify a <em class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">granularity gap</em>: post-training modifies every tensor (zero of 291–339 tensors remain byte-identical, so hash-based deduplication achieves 0% savings), yet preserves block-level structure (mean cosine similarity exceeds 0.99 and relative Frobenius distance stays below 0.13). Post-training therefore acts as a <em class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">structured perturbation</em> that shifts every parameter while leaving block-level geometry intact. The property is not universal: independently trained specializations (e.g., Qwen2.5-Coder) attain cosine similarity <math class="ltx_Math" alttext="\sim" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>∼</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sim</annotation></semantics></math>0.64 with the general base, indicating a disconnected region of weight space. Perturbation magnitude varies systematically with model scale, architecture family, and post-training recipe. As a practical application, we build <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">LinkerLLM</span>, a lazy loader that aliases shareable blocks across co-resident variants, achieving 18–48% GPU memory savings and enabling up to five 7B-parameter variants on a single 24 GB consumer GPU. Five of eight configurations retain <math class="ltx_Math" alttext="\geq" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>≥</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\geq</annotation></semantics></math>94% of the unshared variant’s quality on MMLU, ARC-Challenge, HellaSwag, and WinoGrande; the remaining three (Mistral-7B, Gemma-2-2B, Llama-3.2-1B) have one below-threshold benchmark each (87–91%), which we report transparently rather than gate the block-sharing decision on a single threshold.</p>
</div>
<section id="S1" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">1 </span>Introduction</h2>

<div id="S1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">How do training procedures transform the geometry of neural network weights? Linear mode connectivity <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(Frankle et al., <a href="#bib.bib4" title="" class="ltx_ref">2020</a>; Entezari et al., <a href="#bib.bib3" title="" class="ltx_ref">2022</a>)</cite> established that models fine-tuned from the same initialization remain connected by low-loss paths, and Git Re-Basin <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(Ainsworth et al., <a href="#bib.bib1" title="" class="ltx_ref">2023</a>)</cite> extended this to independently trained models via permutation alignment. We study a specific, practically motivated instance of this question: <em id="S1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">what happens to weight-space structure when a pre-trained LLM undergoes post-training?</em> Post-training (SFT, RLHF, DPO <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(Grattafiori et al., <a href="#bib.bib5" title="" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>; Yang et al., <a href="#bib.bib15" title="" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>)</cite>) produces specialized variants (instruct, chat, code) sharing a common origin.</p>
</div>
<div id="S1.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S1.p2.3" class="ltx_p">Our study reveals a previously undocumented <span id="S1.p2.3.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">granularity gap</span>: post-training perturbs the weight space at the tensor level (every tensor changes; hash-based deduplication is useless) while preserving structure at the block level (cosine <math id="S1.p2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="&gt;" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>&gt;</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">&gt;</annotation></semantics></math>0.99, Frobenius ratio <math id="S1.p2.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="&lt;" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>&lt;</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">&lt;</annotation></semantics></math>0.16 across all eight tested configurations). The property is not universal: independently trained specializations such as Qwen2.5-Coder-7B fall outside the block-connected neighborhood (cos <math id="S1.p2.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\sim" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>∼</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sim</annotation></semantics></math>0.64). We further verify that block-level alignment is <em id="S1.p2.3.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">not</em> a consequence of the architecture’s permutation symmetry: a function-preserving permutation drops the cosine by three orders of magnitude. As a practical application, we build <span id="S1.p2.3.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">LinkerLLM</span>, a runtime loader that aliases shareable blocks across co-resident variants, achieving 18–48% GPU memory savings on 7B-class models on a 24 GB consumer GPU.</p>
</div>
<div id="S1.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S1.p3.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S1.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Related work.</span> The mode connectivity literature characterizes basins at the whole-model level <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(Frankle et al., <a href="#bib.bib4" title="" class="ltx_ref">2020</a>; Entezari et al., <a href="#bib.bib3" title="" class="ltx_ref">2022</a>; Ainsworth et al., <a href="#bib.bib1" title="" class="ltx_ref">2023</a>; Theus et al., <a href="#bib.bib11" title="" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>)</cite>; our analysis refines the picture at the <em id="S1.p3.1.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">block</em> level. Task-vector approaches <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(Ilharco et al., <a href="#bib.bib7" title="" class="ltx_ref">2023</a>; Yadav et al., <a href="#bib.bib14" title="" class="ltx_ref">2023</a>; Yu et al., <a href="#bib.bib17" title="" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>; Wortsman et al., <a href="#bib.bib13" title="" class="ltx_ref">2022</a>; Sun &amp; Dredze, <a href="#bib.bib10" title="" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>)</cite> operate on weight-space <em id="S1.p3.1.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">deltas</em>; we characterize the <em id="S1.p3.1.4" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">structure</em> of those deltas. Multi-model serving systems share LoRA adapters <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(Sheng et al., <a href="#bib.bib9" title="" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>; Chen et al., <a href="#bib.bib2" title="" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>; Hu et al., <a href="#bib.bib6" title="" class="ltx_ref">2022</a>)</cite> or compress deltas <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(Yao et al., <a href="#bib.bib16" title="" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>; Wang et al., <a href="#bib.bib12" title="" class="ltx_ref">2026</a>)</cite>; our empirical finding explains why tensor-hash dedup fails and motivates block-level sharing instead. Concurrent work <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(Zhong &amp; Raghunathan, <a href="#bib.bib19" title="" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>)</cite> reads structure off fine-tuned weights for monitoring and control; our focus is structural quantification and memory-efficient deployment.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S2" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">2 </span>Methodology</h2>

<div id="S2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S2.p1.2" class="ltx_p">For a transformer block <math id="S2.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> with sub-parameters <math id="S2.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{W^{(i)}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msubsup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>k</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>K</mi></msubsup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{W^{(i)}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}</annotation></semantics></math> (attention projections, MLP weights, layernorms), we define two complementary metrics between a base model and a post-trained variant:</p>
<table id="S2.E1" class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table">

<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>

<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td>
<td rowspan="1" class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_eqno ltx_align_middle ltx_align_right"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(2)</span></td>
</tr></tbody>
</table>
<p id="S2.p1.4" class="ltx_p">Cosine measures <em id="S2.p1.4.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">directional</em> preservation; Frobenius ratio measures <em id="S2.p1.4.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">magnitude</em> of perturbation. Both are needed: a block can have high cosine but large Frobenius (e.g., uniform scaling), or vice versa. A block is <em id="S2.p1.4.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">shareable</em> when both pass per-family thresholds:</p>
<table id="S2.E3" class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table">

<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math id="S2.E3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\text{Share}(i)\iff\text{sim}(i)\geq\tau_{\text{cos}}\;\wedge\;\epsilon(i)\leq\tau_{\text{frob}}" display="block" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mtext>Share</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">⇔</mo><mrow><mrow><mtext>sim</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≥</mo><mrow><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>cos</mtext></msub><mo rspace="0.502em">∧</mo><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>≤</mo><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\text{Share}(i)\iff\text{sim}(i)\geq\tau_{\text{cos}}\;\wedge\;\epsilon(i)\leq\tau_{\text{frob}}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td>
<td rowspan="1" class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_eqno ltx_align_middle ltx_align_right"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(3)</span></td>
</tr></tbody>
</table>
<p id="S2.p1.3" class="ltx_p">We study eight base<math id="S2.p1.3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math>instruct pairs across four architecture families (Qwen2.5-0.5/3/7B, Llama-3.2-1/3B, Llama-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, Gemma-2-2B). Weights are compared in float32 after conversion from the model’s native precision; full configuration details appear in Appendix <a href="#A1" title="Appendix A Models and Configurations ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A</span></a>.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S3" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">3 </span>Results</h2>

<section id="S3.SS1" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">3.1 </span>The Granularity Gap</h3>

<div id="S3.SS1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.SS1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Tensor level.</span> For Qwen2.5-7B base vs. instruct, only 2 of 339 tensors are byte-identical (both small bias vectors); for Llama-3.1-8B, zero of 291 match. Post-training produces a <em id="S3.SS1.p1.1.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">dense</em> perturbation, with no tensor left untouched.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.SS1.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS1.p2.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.SS1.p2.2.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Block level.</span> Despite per-tensor changes, transformer blocks retain high similarity (Table <a href="#S3.T1" title="Table 1 ‣ 3.1 The Granularity Gap ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>, Figure <a href="#S3.F1" title="Figure 1 ‣ 3.1 The Granularity Gap ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>): cosine <math id="S3.SS1.p2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="&gt;" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>&gt;</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">&gt;</annotation></semantics></math>0.99 across all eight configurations and Frobenius ratio <math id="S3.SS1.p2.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="&lt;" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>&lt;</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">&lt;</annotation></semantics></math>0.05 for six of eight families. The perturbation is “block-structured”: it modifies every tensor within a block by a small, correlated amount that preserves the block’s function. The block-level mean is representative: per-sub-parameter cosines on Qwen2.5-7B block 14 range from 0.9997 to 1.0000 with std. 0.009 (Appendix <a href="#A7" title="Appendix G Sub-Parameter Aggregation: Mean vs. Max-over-𝐾 ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">G</span></a>).</p>
</div>
<figure id="S3.T1" class="ltx_table">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="S3.T1.4.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 1</span>: </span><span id="S3.T1.5.2" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Block-level similarity between base and instruct variants. <figure id="S3.F1" class="ltx_figure"><img src="2609.26147v1/figures/fig3_frobenius.png" id="S3.F1.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" width="598" height="334" alt="Refer to caption">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="S3.F1.10.5.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 1</span>: </span><span id="S3.F1.8.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Per-block Frobenius ratio <math id="S3.F1.5.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon(i)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon(i)</annotation></semantics></math> across model families (base<math id="S3.F1.6.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math>instruct). Mistral and Qwen families have uniformly low perturbation (all blocks near-frozen). The Llama-3.2 series shows 10–20<math id="S3.F1.7.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>×</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\times</annotation></semantics></math> larger per-block perturbation than Mistral, consistent with more aggressive RLHF; the Llama-3.1-8B variant lies between these regimes (<math id="S3.F1.8.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}=0.045" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0.045</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}=0.045</annotation></semantics></math>), suggesting that perturbation magnitude is not a fixed property of the Llama family but varies with the specific post-training recipe used per release.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<div id="S3.SS1.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS1.p3.9" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.SS1.p3.9.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Correlates of perturbation magnitude.</span> The mean Frobenius ratio <math id="S3.SS1.p3.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}</annotation></semantics></math> varies with (i) model scale (sub-1B Qwen models receive 3–4<math id="S3.SS1.p3.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>×</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\times</annotation></semantics></math> larger perturbation than <math id="S3.SS1.p3.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\geq" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>≥</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\geq</annotation></semantics></math>3B), (ii) training methodology (predominantly-SFT Mistral-v0.3 has <math id="S3.SS1.p3.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}{=}0.006" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0.006</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}{=}0.006</annotation></semantics></math> while heavier-RLHF Llama-3.2 reaches <math id="S3.SS1.p3.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0.110" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0.110</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0.110</annotation></semantics></math>–<math id="S3.SS1.p3.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0.156" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0.156</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0.156</annotation></semantics></math>), and (iii) release vintage (Mistral-v0.1<math id="S3.SS1.p3.7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math>v0.3 and Gemma-1<math id="S3.SS1.p3.8.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math>Gemma-2 show order-of-magnitude tightening between 2023 and 2024 releases, though Llama-2<math id="S3.SS1.p3.9.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math>Llama-3.1 is essentially flat). These correlations are confounded by simultaneous differences in architecture and data; controlled studies would be needed to disentangle them. Full analysis: Appendix <a href="#A9" title="Appendix I Version-over-Version Tightening: Three Family Pairs ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">I</span></a>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.SS1.p4" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS1.p4.14" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.SS1.p4.14.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Perturbation magnitude vs. downstream capability gain.</span> Figure <a href="#S3.F2" title="Figure 2 ‣ 3.1 The Granularity Gap ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a> pairs the per-family <math id="S3.SS1.p4.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}</annotation></semantics></math> with the MMLU gain <math id="S3.SS1.p4.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta\!\text{MMLU}=\text{MMLU}_{\text{instruct}}-\text{MMLU}_{\text{base}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mpadded style="width:0.663em;" width="0.663em"><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mpadded><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mtext>MMLU</mtext></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><msub><mtext>MMLU</mtext><mtext>instruct</mtext></msub><mo>−</mo><msub><mtext>MMLU</mtext><mtext>base</mtext></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta\!\text{MMLU}=\text{MMLU}_{\text{instruct}}-\text{MMLU}_{\text{base}}</annotation></semantics></math> measured under matched lm-eval-harness settings (5-shot, <math id="S3.SS1.p4.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>n</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math>=30 per subject, single seed). Pearson <math id="S3.SS1.p4.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r=0.819" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.819</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r=0.819</annotation></semantics></math> on <math id="S3.SS1.p4.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n=8" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n=8</annotation></semantics></math> families: families with the smallest <math id="S3.SS1.p4.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}</annotation></semantics></math> (Mistral, Qwen-3B, Qwen-7B) show essentially no MMLU change or a small (<math id="S3.SS1.p4.7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="&lt;\!2" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi></mi><mo rspace="0.108em">&lt;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">&lt;\!2</annotation></semantics></math> pt) decrease under instruction-tuning, while families with larger <math id="S3.SS1.p4.8.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}</annotation></semantics></math> (Gemma-2-2B, Llama-3.2-1B, Llama-3.2-3B) accrue <math id="S3.SS1.p4.9.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="5" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>5</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">5</annotation></semantics></math>–<math id="S3.SS1.p4.10.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="9" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>9</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">9</annotation></semantics></math> pt of MMLU gain. We caution against over-interpreting this on <math id="S3.SS1.p4.11.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n=8" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n=8</annotation></semantics></math>: SFT/RLHF objectives do not explicitly optimize MMLU, the <math id="S3.SS1.p4.12.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n=30" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n=30</annotation></semantics></math> subset has <math id="S3.SS1.p4.13.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\pm" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>±</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\pm</annotation></semantics></math>1.2 pt noise, and capability gain on instruction-following benchmarks (IFEval, AlpacaEval) is the more direct training target. The positive correlation does, however, support the qualitative reading that larger weight-space perturbation accompanies more aggressive post-training, which in turn carries more raw-knowledge spillover; the converse—low-<math id="S3.SS1.p4.14.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}</annotation></semantics></math> post-training that nevertheless changes downstream behavior—remains compatible with the data and would be expected for instruction-only fine-tunes whose effects show up at IFEval but not at MMLU.</p>
</div>
<figure id="S3.F2" class="ltx_figure"><img src="2609.26147v1/x1.png" id="S3.F2.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" width="788" height="611" alt="Refer to caption">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="S3.F2.14.7.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 2</span>: </span><span id="S3.F2.12.6" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Per-family perturbation magnitude <math id="S3.F2.7.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}</annotation></semantics></math> vs. MMLU capability gain (Pearson <math id="S3.F2.8.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r=0.819" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.819</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r=0.819</annotation></semantics></math>, <math id="S3.F2.9.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n=8" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n=8</annotation></semantics></math>). Mistral-v0.3 / Qwen-3B / Qwen-7B with <math id="S3.F2.10.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}\in[0.006,0.015]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>0.006</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0.015</mn><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}\in[0.006,0.015]</annotation></semantics></math> show flat or slightly negative MMLU change; larger-<math id="S3.F2.11.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}</annotation></semantics></math> families (Llama-3.2 series, Gemma-2-2B) show 5–9 pt gain. <math id="S3.F2.12.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n=30" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n=30</annotation></semantics></math> per MMLU subject (lm-eval v0.4.11).</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<div id="S3.SS1.p5" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS1.p5.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.SS1.p5.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Error propagation.</span> Per-block perturbations grow at most linearly through the network’s interior (<math id="S3.SS1.p5.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha\in[0.7,2.2]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>0.7</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2.2</mn><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha\in[0.7,2.2]</annotation></semantics></math>) and any output-adjacent amplification is absorbed by the unaliased lm_head/final-norm; downstream quality holds within 96–102% on MMLU/ARC/HellaSwag/WinoGrande for the majority of configurations (Appendix <a href="#A8" title="Appendix H Layer-wise Amplification Factor 𝛼⁢(𝑖) ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">H</span></a>, <a href="#A4" title="Appendix D Downstream Quality Benchmarks ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">D</span></a>).</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S3.SS2" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">3.2 </span>Boundary of Block-Level Connectivity</h3>

<div id="S3.SS2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS2.p1.4" class="ltx_p">Does block-level similarity hold for all variant types? We test the Qwen2.5 base<math id="S3.SS2.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math>instruct pair against four continued-pretrained derivatives of Qwen2.5 base across two specialization branches (Coder, Math) and two model sizes (1.5B, 3B, 7B); results in Table <a href="#S3.T2" title="Table 2 ‣ 3.2 Boundary of Block-Level Connectivity ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>. Across all four continued-pretrained variants the cos drops to 0.52–0.75 and <math id="S3.SS2.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>ϵ</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math> rises to 0.30–0.47—roughly two orders of magnitude looser than the post-trained band (cos <math id="S3.SS2.p1.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="&gt;" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>&gt;</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">&gt;</annotation></semantics></math> 0.99, <math id="S3.SS2.p1.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon&lt;0.05" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mn>0.05</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon&lt;0.05</annotation></semantics></math>).</p>
</div>
<figure id="S3.T2" class="ltx_table">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="S3.T2.12.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 2</span>: </span><span id="S3.T2.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Weight-space distance between variant types within the Qwen2.5 family, all variants derived from the matching Qwen2.5 base. Post-trained (instruct) variants remain in the base’s block-aligned neighborhood across model scales; continued-pretrained Coder/Math derivatives exit it across both branches and both sizes tested, supporting the boundary claim with four independent continued-pretraining examples rather than a single case. Both cos and <math id="S3.T2.2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>ϵ</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math> are means over blocks; model names omit the shared <span id="S3.T2.2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Qwen2.5-</span> prefix.</span></figcaption>

<span id="S3.T2.9.7.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">7B instruct </span><math id="S3.T2.9.7.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo mathsize="0.900em" stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math><span id="S3.T2.9.7.1.2" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;"> Coder-7B</span>
</th>
<td id="S3.T2.9.7.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_border_bb" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><span id="S3.T2.9.7.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">0.637</span></td>
<td id="S3.T2.9.7.3" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_border_bb" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><span id="S3.T2.9.7.3.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">0.831</span></td>
<td id="S3.T2.9.7.4" class="ltx_td ltx_nopad_r ltx_align_center ltx_border_bb" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><span id="S3.T2.9.7.4.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" style="font-size:90%;">Outside</span></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</figure>
<div id="S3.SS2.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS2.p2.1" class="ltx_p">According to its public technical report, Qwen2.5-Coder-7B is itself initialized from the Qwen2.5-7B base and then trained on a code-heavy corpus via continued pretraining plus SFT—it is <em id="S3.SS2.p2.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">not</em> independently initialized. The new four-row evidence in Table <a href="#S3.T2" title="Table 2 ‣ 3.2 Boundary of Block-Level Connectivity ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a> extends this: the same continued-pretraining <math id="S3.SS2.p2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math> outside-the-aligned-neighborhood pattern holds for two independent specialization branches (Coder, Math) and across at least three Qwen2.5 sizes (1.5B, 3B, 7B). This documents an empirical <span id="S3.SS2.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">boundary</span> of the block-aligned neighborhood: continued pretraining of sufficient magnitude exits it, while standard post-training (SFT/RLHF/DPO) does not. We call this a boundary phenomenon rather than a topological theorem, since within a single architecture family (Qwen2.5) we cannot rule out family-specific causes; further extension would require comparable continued-pretrained derivatives from Llama, Mistral, and Gemma families, which we leave to future work.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S3.SS3" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">3.3 </span>Block Similarity is Not Permutation Symmetry</h3>

<div id="S3.SS3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS3.p1.3" class="ltx_p">A natural question for a weight-space symmetries audience is whether block-level alignment is merely a consequence of the architecture’s neuron-permutation group: any permutation <math id="S3.SS3.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\pi" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>π</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\pi</annotation></semantics></math> over the MLP intermediate dimension that acts row-wise on <span id="S3.SS3.p1.3.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">gate_proj</span>/<span id="S3.SS3.p1.3.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">up_proj</span> and column-wise on <span id="S3.SS3.p1.3.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">down_proj</span> leaves the block’s input<math id="S3.SS3.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math>output map identical. The metrics in Eqs. <a href="#S2.E1" title="In 2 Methodology ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>–<a href="#S2.E2" title="In 2 Methodology ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a> are <em id="S3.SS3.p1.3.4" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">not</em> invariant under this symmetry, so the observed <math id="S3.SS3.p1.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="&gt;" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>&gt;</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">&gt;</annotation></semantics></math>0.99 cosine does not follow trivially from architectural equivalence.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.SS3.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS3.p2.4" class="ltx_p">We probe this directly on Qwen2.5-1.5B block 14 (intermediate size 8960). A uniform random permutation <math id="S3.SS3.p2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\pi" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>π</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\pi</annotation></semantics></math> applied to the base block preserves its function exactly (<math id="S3.SS3.p2.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\max|y_{\text{orig}}-y_{\text{perm}}|=9.5{\times}10^{-6}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>max</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><msub><mi>y</mi><mtext>orig</mtext></msub><mo>−</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mtext>perm</mtext></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mn>9.5</mn><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\max|y_{\text{orig}}-y_{\text{perm}}|=9.5{\times}10^{-6}</annotation></semantics></math>, float32 round-off). After permutation, the MLP-mean cosine to the instruct block changes from <math id="S3.SS3.p2.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\cos(\text{base}_{\text{MLP}},\text{instruct}_{\text{MLP}})\approx 1.000" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mtext>base</mtext><mtext>MLP</mtext></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mtext>instruct</mtext><mtext>MLP</mtext></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≈</mo><mn>1.000</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\cos(\text{base}_{\text{MLP}},\text{instruct}_{\text{MLP}})\approx 1.000</annotation></semantics></math> to <math id="S3.SS3.p2.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\cos(\pi{\cdot}\text{base}_{\text{MLP}},\text{instruct}_{\text{MLP}})\approx 0.002" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>π</mi><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">⋅</mo><msub><mtext>base</mtext><mtext>MLP</mtext></msub></mrow><mo>,</mo><msub><mtext>instruct</mtext><mtext>MLP</mtext></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≈</mo><mn>0.002</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\cos(\pi{\cdot}\text{base}_{\text{MLP}},\text{instruct}_{\text{MLP}})\approx 0.002</annotation></semantics></math>: a drop of three orders of magnitude. The result separates two phenomena: (i) the architectural symmetry orbit, which our metric ignores, and (ii) the residual neuron alignment that post-training preserves, which our sharing criterion exploits. If post-training applied an arbitrary element of the permutation group to each block, the criterion would fail; the empirical observation that it does not is the structural property at the heart of this paper.</p>
</div>
<section id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Extending the control to attention heads and to RMSNorm rescaling.</h4>

<div id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.7" class="ltx_p">Beyond the MLP-intermediate symmetry, transformer blocks admit (a) a head-permutation symmetry on the attention sub-block—permuting groups of <span id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.7.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">head_dim</span> rows of Q/K/V in lock-step with the same permutation on <span id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.7.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">head_dim</span>-column groups of the output projection is function-preserving (with care for grouped-query attention)—and (b) an RMSNorm channel-rescaling symmetry—scaling <span id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">input_layernorm.weight<math id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="[i]\!\leftarrow\!c_{i}\!\cdot\!\texttt{weight}[i]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0.108em" rspace="0.108em" stretchy="false">←</mo><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.052em">⋅</mo><mtext class="ltx_mathvariant_monospace">weight</mtext></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">[i]\!\leftarrow\!c_{i}\!\cdot\!\texttt{weight}[i]</annotation></semantics></math></span> and dividing the next-layer Q/K/V input columns by <math id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>. We apply both as a control on one block of Qwen2.5-1.5B (block 14), Qwen2.5-7B (block 14), and Llama-3.1-8B (block 16); results in Table <a href="#S3.T3" title="Table 3 ‣ Extending the control to attention heads and to RMSNorm rescaling. ‣ 3.3 Block Similarity is Not Permutation Symmetry ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>. A random head permutation on the variant drops attention-mean cosine from 0.999–1.000 to 0.07–0.15 across all three models—a 7–14<math id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.3.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>×</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\times</annotation></semantics></math> drop confirming the high attention-block similarity is not a head-permutation artifact. A modest RMSNorm rescaling (<math id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.4.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c_{i}\sim\text{Uniform}[0.5,2.0]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>∼</mo><mrow><mtext>Uniform</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>0.5</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2.0</mn><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c_{i}\sim\text{Uniform}[0.5,2.0]</annotation></semantics></math>) drops attention-mean cosine only mildly (<math id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.5.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\sim" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>∼</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sim</annotation></semantics></math>0.97), but inflates the symmetric Frobenius ratio by 7–23<math id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.6.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>×</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\times</annotation></semantics></math>, so any sub-model basin claim that relied on <math id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1.7.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>ϵ</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math> as the sole metric would fail under RMSNorm rescaling. We therefore report both metrics throughout.</p>
</div>
<figure id="S3.T3" class="ltx_table">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="S3.T3.8.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 3</span>: </span><span id="S3.T3.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Permutation/rescaling controls on three families (one block per model). <span id="S3.T3.2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">ref</span> = no perturbation; <span id="S3.T3.2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">head_perm</span> = random function-preserving head permutation on the variant; <span id="S3.T3.2.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">rmsnorm</span> = random channel rescaling <math id="S3.T3.2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c_{i}\!\sim\!\mathcal{U}[0.5,2.0]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo lspace="0.108em" rspace="0.108em">∼</mo><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒰</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>0.5</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2.0</mn><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c_{i}\!\sim\!\mathcal{U}[0.5,2.0]</annotation></semantics></math> on the variant. </section>
<section id="S3.SS4" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">3.4 </span>Loss-Along-Interpolation: Block-Level Path Test</h3>

<div id="S3.SS4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS4.p1.5" class="ltx_p">To turn the parameter-proximity observation into a loss-landscape statement, we measure the actual LM cross-entropy loss along linear weight-space paths. For each transformer block <math id="S3.SS4.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> we set the variant’s block <math id="S3.SS4.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> to <math id="S3.SS4.p1.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="W_{i}(\alpha)=\alpha W_{i}^{\text{base}}+(1-\alpha)\,W_{i}^{\text{var}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>α</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>i</mi><mtext>base</mtext></msubsup></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mi>α</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0.170em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>i</mi><mtext>var</mtext></msubsup></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{i}(\alpha)=\alpha W_{i}^{\text{base}}+(1-\alpha)\,W_{i}^{\text{var}}</annotation></semantics></math> for <math id="S3.SS4.p1.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha\in\{0,0.1,\dots,1.0\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0.1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>1.0</mn><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha\in\{0,0.1,\dots,1.0\}</annotation></semantics></math>, keep every other block at variant values, and compute wikitext-2 LM loss (24 sequences <math id="S3.SS4.p1.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>×</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\times</annotation></semantics></math> 512 tokens, lm-eval-harness style). We also measure the <em id="S3.SS4.p1.5.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">full-model</em> interpolation where all blocks are interpolated jointly. Sub-model linear mode connectivity at the block level predicts barrier-free per-block paths.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.SS4.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS4.p2.2" class="ltx_p">We evaluate the protocol on three populations: (i) four representative post-trained families spanning 1.5B–8B parameters and three architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, Mistral-7B, Llama-3.1-8B); (ii) the panel boundary family Llama-3.2-1B; (iii) two continued-pretrained derivatives of the same Qwen2.5-1.5B base (Qwen2.5-Coder-1.5B and Qwen2.5-Math-1.5B). Table <a href="#A12.T13" title="Table 13 ‣ Appendix L Loss-Along-Interpolation: Full Per-Family Data ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">13</span></a> and Figure <a href="#S3.F3" title="Figure 3 ‣ 3.4 Loss-Along-Interpolation: Block-Level Path Test ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> report per-block max <math id="S3.SS4.p2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta L" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta L</annotation></semantics></math> and full-model peak <math id="S3.SS4.p2.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta L" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta L</annotation></semantics></math> across these seven configurations.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.SS4.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS4.p3.5" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.SS4.p3.5.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Result on the post-trained band (4 families).</span> For every post-trained family the per-block paths are essentially flat: per-block max <math id="S3.SS4.p3.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta L\leq 0.0062" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mn>0.0062</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta L\leq 0.0062</annotation></semantics></math> nats across all 28–32 blocks per family (median over the 120 blocks: <math id="S3.SS4.p3.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0.0000" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0.0000</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0.0000</annotation></semantics></math> nats). The full-model interpolation is also barrier-free: <math id="S3.SS4.p3.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="L_{\text{full}}(\alpha)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>L</mi><mtext>full</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>α</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">L_{\text{full}}(\alpha)</annotation></semantics></math> <em id="S3.SS4.p3.5.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">decreases</em> monotonically from variant to base on Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, Mistral-7B, and Llama-3.1-8B—the base ends are <math id="S3.SS4.p3.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0.04" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0.04</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0.04</annotation></semantics></math>–<math id="S3.SS4.p3.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0.09" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0.09</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0.09</annotation></semantics></math> nats below the variant. The data therefore satisfy both a sub-model and a full-model linear-mode-connectivity criterion on every post-trained family we tested: the criterion is not a parameter-proximity surrogate.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.SS4.p4" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS4.p4.5" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.SS4.p4.5.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Result on the boundary band.</span> The same protocol on the panel’s boundary family Llama-3.2-1B (<math id="S3.SS4.p4.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}=0.156" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0.156</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}=0.156</annotation></semantics></math>) gives a qualitatively different picture: block 0 (embedding-adjacent) has a per-block barrier of <math id="S3.SS4.p4.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\approx" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>≈</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\approx</annotation></semantics></math>0.15 nats, block 15 (lm_head-adjacent) has a much larger <math id="S3.SS4.p4.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\approx" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>≈</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\approx</annotation></semantics></math>2.76-nat barrier, while the interior blocks 2–14 remain flat. The full-model path also rises by <math id="S3.SS4.p4.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\approx 2.68" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi></mi><mo>≈</mo><mn>2.68</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\approx 2.68</annotation></semantics></math> nats from variant to base. This explains the conservative <math id="S3.SS4.p4.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}{=}0.20" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.20</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}{=}0.20</annotation></semantics></math> + restricted share-set Llama-3.2-1B requires in Table <a href="#S4.T4" title="Table 4 ‣ 4 Application: LinkerLLM ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>: per-block geometric similarity is necessary but not sufficient on this family; the geometric criterion correctly excludes exactly the two boundary blocks that the loss-interpolation protocol identifies.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.SS4.p5" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS4.p5.7" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.SS4.p5.7.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Result on continued-pretrained derivatives.</span> Continued-pretrained derivatives of Qwen2.5-1.5B—Coder-1.5B and Math-1.5B—fail both the sub-model and the full-model linear-mode-connectivity tests dramatically. Per-block max <math id="S3.SS4.p5.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta L" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta L</annotation></semantics></math> reaches <math id="S3.SS4.p5.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="9.09" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>9.09</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">9.09</annotation></semantics></math> nats (Coder block 0) and <math id="S3.SS4.p5.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="6.52" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>6.52</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">6.52</annotation></semantics></math> nats (Math block 0); 27 of 28 Coder blocks have per-block barriers above <math id="S3.SS4.p5.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0.1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0.1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0.1</annotation></semantics></math> nats. The full-model interpolation rises by <math id="S3.SS4.p5.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="6.7" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>6.7</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">6.7</annotation></semantics></math>–<math id="S3.SS4.p5.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="7.8" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>7.8</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">7.8</annotation></semantics></math> nats from variant to base. Continued-pretrained derivatives are therefore quantitatively a separate population from post-trained instruct variants, not merely “further along the same axis”: they exit the loss-aligned neighborhood entirely. This is consistent with the geometric boundary they cross in Table <a href="#S3.T2" title="Table 2 ‣ 3.2 Boundary of Block-Level Connectivity ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a> (cos <math id="S3.SS4.p5.7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\leq 0.75" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi></mi><mo>≤</mo><mn>0.75</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\leq 0.75</annotation></semantics></math>).</p>
</div>
<figure id="S3.F3" class="ltx_figure"><img src="2609.26147v1/x2.png" id="S3.F3.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" width="830" height="304" alt="Refer to caption">

Blue = four post-trained families; orange = boundary family Llama-3.2-1B; pink = two continued-pretrained derivatives. </figure>
<figure id="S3.F4" class="ltx_figure"><img src="2609.26147v1/x3.png" id="S3.F4.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" width="790" height="565" alt="Refer to caption">

Each grey curve is a per-block path <math id="S3.F4.5.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta L_{i}(\alpha)=L_{i}(\alpha)-L_{\text{variant}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>α</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>α</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>−</mo><msub><mi>L</mi><mtext>variant</mtext></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta L_{i}(\alpha)=L_{i}(\alpha)-L_{\text{variant}}</annotation></semantics></math>. Orange: full-model interpolation <math id="S3.F4.7.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="L(\alpha)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>α</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">L(\alpha)</annotation></semantics></math>, also barrier-free. Compare with Llama-3.2-1B (boundary family, Appendix <a href="#A12" title="Appendix L Loss-Along-Interpolation: Full Per-Family Data ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">L</span></a>) where block 0 develops a <math id="S3.F4.8.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\sim" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>∼</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sim</annotation></semantics></math>0.15-nat per-block barrier.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
</section>
<section id="S4" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">4 </span>Application: LinkerLLM</h2>

<div id="S4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p1.6" class="ltx_p">Block-level persistence has a direct systems application. Given a donor model <math id="S4.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math> already on GPU and a recipient model <math id="S4.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>B</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B</annotation></semantics></math>, LinkerLLM’s <em id="S4.p1.6.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">aliasing primitive</em> executes <span id="S4.p1.6.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">param_B.data = param_A.data</span> on shareable blocks: a PyTorch storage alias with no data copy. To avoid the <math id="S4.p1.3.m3" class="ltx_math_unparsed" alttext="2{\times}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0.222em">×</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2{\times}</annotation></semantics></math> peak memory of naïve dual-loading, our <em id="S4.p1.6.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">lazy loader</em> keeps <math id="S4.p1.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>B</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B</annotation></semantics></math> on CPU during the similarity scan and moves only its non-aliased parameters (embed, norm, lm_head) to GPU. Peak GPU memory becomes <math id="S4.p1.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="|A|+|\text{unique}(B)|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mtext>unique</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">|A|+|\text{unique}(B)|</annotation></semantics></math> rather than <math id="S4.p1.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="|A|+|B|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">|A|+|B|</annotation></semantics></math> (Appendix <a href="#A2" title="Appendix B Lazy Loader: Algorithm and Architecture ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">B</span></a> for the algorithm and architecture diagram).</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p2.3" class="ltx_p">Table <a href="#S4.T4" title="Table 4 ‣ 4 Application: LinkerLLM ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a> reports GPU memory for two co-resident variants on an RTX 3090. Six of eight configurations achieve 32–48% savings; the flat peak enables Qwen2.5-7B and Llama-3.1-8B 2-variant configurations that would otherwise OOM on 24 GB. Scaling improves with <math id="S4.p2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>N</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N</annotation></semantics></math>: Mistral-7B reaches 5<math id="S4.p2.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>×</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\times</annotation></semantics></math> 7B variants in 15.5 GB (77% saving) on a single 24 GB card (Appendix <a href="#A3" title="Appendix C 𝑁-Variant Memory Scaling ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">C</span></a>). Quality retention on MMLU/ARC-Challenge/HellaSwag/WinoGrande is <math id="S4.p2.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\geq" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>≥</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\geq</annotation></semantics></math>94% on every benchmark for five of eight configurations (Appendix <a href="#A4" title="Appendix D Downstream Quality Benchmarks ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">D</span></a>).</p>
</div>
<figure id="S4.T4" class="ltx_table">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="S4.T4.8.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 4</span>: </span><span id="S4.T4.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">GPU memory (MiB) for two co-resident variants (base + instruct) on RTX 3090. <sup id="S4.T4.2.1.1" class="ltx_sup">∗</sup>Naive would OOM on 24 GB.</span></figcaption>
<table id="S4.T4.5" class="ltx_tabular ltx_centering ltx_guessed_headers ltx_align_middle">
<thead class="ltx_thead">
<tr id="S4.T4.3.1" class="ltx_tr">
<th id="S4.T4.3.1.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_th ltx_th_column ltx_border_tt" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><span id="S4.T4.3.1.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Family</span></th>
<th id="S4.T4.3.1.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_th ltx_th_column ltx_border_tt" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><math id="S4.T4.3.1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi mathsize="0.900em">τ</mi><mtext mathsize="0.900em">frob</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}</annotation></semantics></math></th>
<th id="S4.T4.3.1.3" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_th ltx_th_column ltx_border_tt" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><span id="S4.T4.3.1.3.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Shared</span></th>
<th id="S4.T4.3.1.4" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_th ltx_th_column ltx_border_tt" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><span id="S4.T4.3.1.4.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Naive</span></th>
<th id="S4.T4.3.1.5" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_th ltx_th_column ltx_border_tt" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><span id="S4.T4.3.1.5.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Lazy</span></th>
<th id="S4.T4.3.1.6" class="ltx_td ltx_nopad_r ltx_align_center ltx_th ltx_th_column ltx_border_tt" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><span id="S4.T4.3.1.6.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Save</span></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody class="ltx_tbody">
<tr id="S4.T4.5.4.1" class="ltx_tr">
<td id="S4.T4.5.4.1.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_border_t" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><span id="S4.T4.5.4.1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Mistral-7B</span></td>

<section id="S5" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">5 </span>Discussion and Conclusion</h2>

<div id="S5.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.p1.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.p1.2.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Relation to mode connectivity.</span> Our analysis measures <em id="S5.p1.2.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">parameter-space proximity</em> between trained variants, not loss-barrier connectivity along an explicit interpolation path. We therefore frame the contribution as a <em id="S5.p1.2.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">block-level parameter alignment</em> observation that is suggestive of, but does not by itself establish, sub-model linear-mode connectivity in the sense of <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citet">Frankle et al. (<a href="#bib.bib4" title="" class="ltx_ref">2020</a>)</cite>. With that caveat, the data suggest that post-trained variants remain in a tight <em id="S5.p1.2.4" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">parameter</em> neighborhood at the block level (cos <math id="S5.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="&gt;" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>&gt;</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">&gt;</annotation></semantics></math>0.99) while the full-model deltas accumulate, consistent with a hierarchical structure that future work could test directly by measuring loss along block-wise interpolation paths. We hypothesize two contributing mechanisms: (i) <em id="S5.p1.2.5" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">gradient locality</em>, since post-training objectives primarily modify the input–output mapping and gradient signals attenuate at intermediate blocks; (ii) <em id="S5.p1.2.6" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">functional redundancy</em>, since blocks are over-parameterized for the small perturbation that SFT/RLHF introduces, consistent with DARE <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(Yu et al., <a href="#bib.bib17" title="" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>)</cite>. The simplest gradient-magnitude form of (i) is in fact falsified for Gemma-2-2B (Appendix <a href="#A17" title="Appendix Q Gradient-Locality Probe ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Q</span></a>): the dominant predictor of block divergence is depth, not gradient norm. The contrast in Table <a href="#S3.T2" title="Table 2 ‣ 3.2 Boundary of Block-Level Connectivity ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a> (Qwen2.5-Coder-7B at cos <math id="S5.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\sim" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>∼</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sim</annotation></semantics></math>0.64 against Qwen2.5 base) is empirically a <em id="S5.p1.2.7" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">stronger</em> claim than “independent vs. shared initialization,” since Qwen2.5-Coder-7B is itself derived from the Qwen2.5 base via continued pretraining: the data therefore suggest that continued pretraining of sufficient magnitude can also exit the block-aligned neighborhood, not just from-scratch independent training. <span id="S5.p1.2.8" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Limitations.</span> We study 0.5–8B models on consumer GPUs (70B validation requires datacenter hardware); the lazy loader trades 22–56 s of CPU similarity scan for memory; integration with production engines such as vLLM <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(Kwon et al., <a href="#bib.bib8" title="" class="ltx_ref">2023</a>)</cite> and SGLang <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(Zheng et al., <a href="#bib.bib18" title="" class="ltx_ref">2023</a>)</cite> requires single-engine multi-variant routing, which we leave to future work.</p>
</div>
<div id="S5.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.p2.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Conclusion.</span> Post-training perturbs every tensor but preserves block-level parameter alignment across four LLM families: a previously undocumented granularity gap in weight space. This finding is suggestive of a sub-model extension of the mode-connectivity picture—rigorously testable by direct loss-along-interpolation measurement, which we leave to future work—and yields immediate practical value through LinkerLLM (18–48% GPU memory savings, up to 5<math id="S5.p2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>×</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\times</annotation></semantics></math> 7B variants on a single consumer GPU).</p>
</div>
</section>

<section id="A1" class="ltx_appendix">

<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix A </span>Models and Configurations</h2>

<div id="A1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We study eight base<math id="A1.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math>instruct pairs spanning four architecture families (Table <a href="#A1.T5" title="Table 5 ‣ Appendix A Models and Configurations ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a>).
