<section id="A2" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix B </span>Lazy Loader: Algorithm and Architecture</h2>

<figure id="alg1" class="ltx_float ltx_float_algorithm ltx_framed ltx_framed_top">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_float"><span id="alg1.2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Algorithm 1</span> </span> Lazy Loader Pipeline</figcaption>
<div id="alg1.3" class="ltx_listing ltx_listing">
<div id="alg1.l0" class="ltx_listingline">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg1.l0.1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">0:</span></span> Donor model <math id="alg1.l0.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math> on GPU, recipient model ID <math id="alg1.l0.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>B</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B</annotation></semantics></math>

</div>
<div id="alg1.l0a" class="ltx_listingline">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg1.l0a.1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">0:</span></span> Thresholds <math id="alg1.l0a.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{cos}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>cos</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{cos}}</annotation></semantics></math>, <math id="alg1.l0a.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}</annotation></semantics></math>

</div>
<div id="alg1.l1" class="ltx_listingline">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg1.l1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">1:</span></span> Load <math id="alg1.l1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>B</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B</annotation></semantics></math> to <span id="alg1.l1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">CPU only</span> (no GPU allocation)

</div>
<div id="alg1.l2" class="ltx_listingline">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg1.l2.1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">2:</span></span> Dry-run scan: compute <math id="alg1.l2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\text{sim}(i)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mtext>sim</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\text{sim}(i)</annotation></semantics></math>, <math id="alg1.l2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon(i)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon(i)</annotation></semantics></math> per block on CPU

</div>
<div id="alg1.l3" class="ltx_listingline">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg1.l3.2.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">3:</span></span> <span id="alg1.l3.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Alias</span> shareable blocks: <span id="alg1.l3.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">param_B.data <math id="alg1.l3.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\leftarrow" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">←</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\leftarrow</annotation></semantics></math> param_A.data</span>

</div>
<div id="alg1.l4" class="ltx_listingline">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg1.l4.1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">4:</span></span> <span id="alg1.l4.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Move</span> only non-aliased params (embed, norm, lm_head) to GPU

</div>
<div id="alg1.l4a" class="ltx_listingline">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg1.l4a.1.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">4:</span></span> Peak GPU <math id="alg1.l4a.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="=|A|+|\text{unique}(B)|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi></mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mtext>unique</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">=|A|+|\text{unique}(B)|</annotation></semantics></math>, not <math id="alg1.l4a.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="|A|+|B|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">|A|+|B|</annotation></semantics></math>

</div>
</div>
</figure>
<figure id="A2.F5" class="ltx_figure"><img src="2609.26147v1/figures/fig1_architecture.png" id="A2.F5.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" width="598" height="334" alt="Refer to caption">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A2.F5.4.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 5</span>: </span><span id="A2.F5.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">LinkerLLM lazy loader. Donor model A resides on GPU. Recipient B is loaded to CPU, similarity-scanned, and only unique sections are moved to GPU. Shared blocks alias donor’s storage directly. Peak GPU <math id="A2.F5.2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="=|A|+|\text{unique}(B)|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi></mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mtext>unique</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">=|A|+|\text{unique}(B)|</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="A3" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix C </span><math id="A3.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>N</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N</annotation></semantics></math>-Variant Memory Scaling</h2>

<div id="A3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A3.p1.3" class="ltx_p">LinkerLLM’s advantage grows with the number of co-resident variants <math id="A3.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>N</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N</annotation></semantics></math>: shared blocks are loaded once, and each additional variant contributes only its unique sections (Figure <a href="#A3.F6" title="Figure 6 ‣ Appendix C 𝑁-Variant Memory Scaling ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6</span></a>). Mistral-7B reaches 5<math id="A3.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>×</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\times</annotation></semantics></math> 7B variants in 15.5 GB (77% saving) on a single 24 GB card; Qwen2.5-7B reaches 4<math id="A3.p1.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>×</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\times</annotation></semantics></math> in 20.3 GB (64% saving).</p>
</div>
<figure id="A3.F6" class="ltx_figure"><img src="2609.26147v1/figures/fig2_memory_scaling.png" id="A3.F6.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" width="598" height="402" alt="Refer to caption">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A3.F6.6.3.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 6</span>: </span><span id="A3.F6.4.2" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Memory scaling with <math id="A3.F6.3.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>N</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N</annotation></semantics></math> variants (Qwen2.5-7B, fp16). LinkerLLM grows at <math id="A3.F6.4.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\sim" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>∼</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sim</annotation></semantics></math>2 GB/variant vs. 14.2 GB/variant for independent loading.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<div id="A3.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A3.p2.4" class="ltx_p"><span id="A3.p2.4.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Relationship to quantization.</span> Quantization is an orthogonal axis: loading <math id="A3.p2.1.m1" class="ltx_math_unparsed" alttext="5\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo lspace="0.222em">×</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">5\times</annotation></semantics></math> Mistral-7B in AWQ 4-bit independently costs <math id="A3.p2.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="5\times 3.5=17.5" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><mn>3.5</mn></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>17.5</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">5\times 3.5=17.5</annotation></semantics></math> GB, comparable to LinkerLLM’s 15.5 GB in fp16. The two approaches <em id="A3.p2.4.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">compose</em>: applying LinkerLLM to quantized variants would share 4-bit blocks across variants, reducing the <math id="A3.p2.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>N</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N</annotation></semantics></math>-variant cost to <math id="A3.p2.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="3.5+0.25\times(N{-}1)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>3.5</mn><mo>+</mo><mrow><mn>0.25</mn><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">3.5+0.25\times(N{-}1)</annotation></semantics></math> GB. We focus on fp16 to isolate the sharing mechanism from quantization effects.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="A4" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix D </span>Downstream Quality Benchmarks</h2>

<div id="A4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A4.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We evaluate quality retention on MMLU, ARC-Challenge, HellaSwag, and WinoGrande using lm-eval-harness v0.4.11 (Tables <a href="#A4.T6" title="Table 6 ‣ Appendix D Downstream Quality Benchmarks ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6</span></a>, <a href="#A4.T7" title="Table 7 ‣ Appendix D Downstream Quality Benchmarks ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">7</span></a>). Quality numbers are measured by replacing shareable transformer blocks with the base model’s blocks and re-evaluating the variant, the same substitution performed by the aliasing primitive. Five of eight configurations retain <math id="A4.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\geq" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>≥</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\geq</annotation></semantics></math>94% on every benchmark; the remaining three configurations have a single below-threshold cell each: Mistral-7B on ARC-Challenge (51.7 vs. 59.3, 87%), Gemma-2-2B on MMLU (51.7 vs. 56.9, 91%), and Llama-3.2-1B on MMLU (39.9 vs. 48.3, 83% at conservative sharing). Table <a href="#A4.T7" title="Table 7 ‣ Appendix D Downstream Quality Benchmarks ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">7</span></a> gives the underlying absolute scores so the reader can judge whether the shared variant remains task-useful in absolute terms even when relative retention dips below the 94% threshold (e.g., Gemma-2-2B shared MMLU 51.7% remains well above the random-baseline 25%).</p>
</div>
<figure id="A4.T6" class="ltx_table">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A4.T6.11.3.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 6</span>: </span><span id="A4.T6.4.2" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Quality retained (%) after sharing at per-family optimal thresholds. The aggregation used here (cosine-of-concatenated-sub-parameters) differs from the deployed mean-of-per-sub-parameter-cosines in <span id="A4.T6.4.2.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">aliasing.py</span>; the two agree for homogeneous-perturbation families (Mistral, Qwen) but differ for heterogeneous-perturbation families. <sup id="A4.T6.4.2.2" class="ltx_sup">†</sup>Llama-3.2-3B at <math id="A4.T6.4.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{cos}}{=}0.999" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>cos</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.999</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{cos}}{=}0.999</annotation></semantics></math> shares 5/28 blocks (vs. 28/28 in Table <a href="#S4.T4" title="Table 4 ‣ 4 Application: LinkerLLM ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>); Llama-3.2-1B shares 6/16 (vs. 13/16); savings and quality reflect this more conservative sharing. The Gemma-2-2B row uses the deployed mean-of-cosines criterion (12/26 shared, matching Table <a href="#S4.T4" title="Table 4 ‣ 4 Application: LinkerLLM ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>). Sharing produces <em id="A4.T6.4.2.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">deterministic</em> outputs but token-level greedy decoding may diverge from the unshared variant after a few tokens due to autoregressive amplification.</span></figcaption>

<section id="A5" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix E </span>Automatic Threshold Selection</h2>

<div id="A5.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A5.p1.4" class="ltx_p">The optimal <math id="A5.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}</annotation></semantics></math> varies by family (Table <a href="#S3.T1" title="Table 1 ‣ 3.1 The Granularity Gap ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>). We implement an automatic sweep that, given a quality budget (maximum acceptable quality drop), selects the widest <math id="A5.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}</annotation></semantics></math> using a Frobenius-to-quality heuristic calibrated on downstream benchmarks. For Qwen2.5-0.5B with a 5% quality budget, the tuner selects <math id="A5.p1.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}{=}0.06" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.06</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}{=}0.06</annotation></semantics></math> (24/24 blocks shareable); for Llama-3.2-1B, a 5% budget yields no sharing, while 10% yields <math id="A5.p1.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}{=}0.13" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.13</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}{=}0.13</annotation></semantics></math> (13/16 blocks).</p>
</div>
<section id="A5.SS0.SSS0.Px1" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Cost-effective threshold selection in practice.</h4>

<div id="A5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">The threshold scan is not amortized over many serving requests by itself, so its CPU cost (<math id="A5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\sim" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>∼</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sim</annotation></semantics></math>22–56 s per family, Appendix <a href="#A14" title="Appendix N Cold-Start Latency Breakdown ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">N</span></a>) must be paid once per (donor, recipient) pair. We recommend the following default workflow when adding a new variant to a deployed registry:</p>
<ol id="A5.I1" class="ltx_enumerate">
<li id="A5.I1.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">1.</span> 
<div id="A5.I1.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A5.I1.i1.p1.3" class="ltx_p"><em id="A5.I1.i1.p1.3.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">Cold-default tier.</em> Set <math id="A5.I1.i1.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(\tau_{\text{cos}},\tau_{\text{frob}})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>cos</mtext></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(\tau_{\text{cos}},\tau_{\text{frob}})</annotation></semantics></math> to the family’s literature default if the (architecture, post-training recipe) is recognized, otherwise to the conservative tier <math id="A5.I1.i1.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(\tau_{\text{cos}}{=}0.999,\tau_{\text{frob}}{=}0.05)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>cos</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.999</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.05</mn></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(\tau_{\text{cos}}{=}0.999,\tau_{\text{frob}}{=}0.05)</annotation></semantics></math>. This safely captures the post-training band identified in Table <a href="#S3.T1" title="Table 1 ‣ 3.1 The Granularity Gap ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> (<math id="A5.I1.i1.p1.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}&lt;0.05" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>&lt;</mo><mn>0.05</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}&lt;0.05</annotation></semantics></math> for 6 of 8 families) at the cost of leaving boundary-family savings on the table.</p>
</div>
</li>
<li id="A5.I1.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">2.</span> 
<div id="A5.I1.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A5.I1.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><em id="A5.I1.i2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">Empirical refinement.</em> If serving load justifies it, run the auto-tuner once with a 5%-quality budget on PIQA (the cheapest of the four held-out benchmarks); accept the widest <math id="A5.I1.i2.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}</annotation></semantics></math> that holds. The tuner’s PIQA-to-held-out generalization within the post-training band is documented in Appendix <a href="#A15" title="Appendix O Generation Quality Under Aggressive Sharing ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">O</span></a>.</p>
</div>
</li>
<li id="A5.I1.i3" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">3.</span> 
<div id="A5.I1.i3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A5.I1.i3.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><em id="A5.I1.i3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">Boundary detection.</em> If <math id="A5.I1.i3.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}&gt;0.10" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0.10</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}&gt;0.10</annotation></semantics></math> from the scan, treat the family as a boundary case: drop the empirical refinement (it over-promises for Llama-3.2-1B by 8.9 pts on held-out tasks) and stay at the conservative tier.</p>
</div>
</li>
</ol>
<p id="A5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.3" class="ltx_p">The scan cost is fully amortized after a single eviction-and-reload cycle of the recipient variant: a 7B variant weighs 14 GB on disk, while the scan reads <math id="A5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\sim" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>∼</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sim</annotation></semantics></math>2 GB block-by-block, so the I/O budget for the scan is <math id="A5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.3.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\sim" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>∼</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sim</annotation></semantics></math>15% of one full reload. In production registries that already maintain per-checkpoint summary statistics (vintage, recipe class, base lineage), the cold-default tier is the dominant case and the scan is invoked only at registry-onboarding time.</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>

<section id="A7" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix G </span>Sub-Parameter Aggregation: Mean vs. Max-over-<math id="A7.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="K" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>K</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">K</annotation></semantics></math>
</h2>

<div id="A7.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A7.p1.4" class="ltx_p">Tables <a href="#S3.T1" title="Table 1 ‣ 3.1 The Granularity Gap ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> and <a href="#A6.T8" title="Table 8 ‣ Appendix F Per-Family Frobenius Distribution ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">8</span></a> aggregate the per-block Frobenius ratio by averaging over the <math id="A7.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="K" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>K</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">K</annotation></semantics></math> sub-parameters of each block (Eq. <a href="#S2.E2" title="In 2 Methodology ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>). A natural concern is whether this mean-over-<math id="A7.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="K" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>K</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">K</annotation></semantics></math> aggregation hides a divergent sub-parameter (e.g., a single attention head perturbed at <math id="A7.p1.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon=0.30" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.30</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon=0.30</annotation></semantics></math> inside a block whose other components are near-frozen). For three families with full per-sub-parameter <math id="A7.p1.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta</annotation></semantics></math>-decomposition data, we recompute the worst-case statistics in Table <a href="#A7.T9" title="Table 9 ‣ Appendix G Sub-Parameter Aggregation: Mean vs. Max-over-𝐾 ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">9</span></a>.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A7.T9" class="ltx_table">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A7.T9.20.8.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 9</span>: </span><span id="A7.T9.14.7" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Mean-over-<math id="A7.T9.8.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="K" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>K</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">K</annotation></semantics></math> vs. max-over-<math id="A7.T9.9.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="K" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>K</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">K</annotation></semantics></math> aggregation of relative Frobenius perturbation. “mean <math id="A7.T9.10.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}</annotation></semantics></math>” is the mean across blocks of the per-block mean-over-sub-parameters (the value reported in Table <a href="#A6.T8" title="Table 8 ‣ Appendix F Per-Family Frobenius Distribution ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">8</span></a>). “max-<math id="A7.T9.11.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="K" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>K</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">K</annotation></semantics></math> mean” is the mean across blocks of the per-block <em id="A7.T9.14.7.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">maximum</em> sub-parameter <math id="A7.T9.12.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>ϵ</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>. “global max” is the maximum over all (block, sub-parameter) pairs. For all three families, even the global max stays below the deployed <math id="A7.T9.13.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}</annotation></semantics></math>, confirming that mean-over-<math id="A7.T9.14.7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="K" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>K</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">K</annotation></semantics></math> does not hide outlier sub-parameters.</span></figcaption>
<table id="A7.T9.17" class="ltx_tabular ltx_centering ltx_guessed_headers ltx_align_middle">
<thead class="ltx_thead">
<tr id="A7.T9.17.3" class="ltx_tr">

<th id="A7.T9.15.1.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_th ltx_th_column ltx_border_tt" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><math id="A7.T9.15.1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi mathsize="0.900em">τ</mi><mtext mathsize="0.900em">frob</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}</annotation></semantics></math></th>
<th id="A7.T9.16.2.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_th ltx_th_column ltx_border_tt" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;">
<span id="A7.T9.16.2.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">mean </span><math id="A7.T9.16.2.2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><mi mathsize="0.900em">ϵ</mi><mo mathsize="0.900em">¯</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}</annotation></semantics></math>

<span id="A7.T9.17.3.3.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">max-</span><math id="A7.T9.17.3.3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="K" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi mathsize="0.900em">K</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">K</annotation></semantics></math><span id="A7.T9.17.3.3.2" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;"> mean</span>

<div id="A7.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A7.p2.4" class="ltx_p">The max-over-<math id="A7.p2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="K" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>K</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">K</annotation></semantics></math> ratio is consistently <math id="A7.p2.2.m2" class="ltx_math_unparsed" alttext="1.5\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1.5</mn><mo lspace="0.222em">×</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1.5\times</annotation></semantics></math> the mean and the global max is at most <math id="A7.p2.3.m3" class="ltx_math_unparsed" alttext="1.8\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1.8</mn><mo lspace="0.222em">×</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1.8\times</annotation></semantics></math>, but neither violates the per-family <math id="A7.p2.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}</annotation></semantics></math>. Equivalently, the dual-metric criterion in Eq. <a href="#S2.E3" title="In 2 Methodology ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>, defended on a single Qwen2.5-7B block in §<a href="#S3" title="3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>, generalizes: no sub-parameter within any shareable block is a hidden outlier.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="A8" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix H </span>Layer-wise Amplification Factor <math id="A8.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha(i)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha(i)</annotation></semantics></math>
</h2>

<div id="A8.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A8.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Figure <a href="#A8.F7" title="Figure 7 ‣ Appendix H Layer-wise Amplification Factor 𝛼⁢(𝑖) ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">7</span></a> contrasts the per-layer amplification factor measured on Qwen2.5-1.5B and Qwen2.5-3B (100 inputs, fp32 forward pass with shared blocks vs. unshared baseline). The shape is qualitatively the same in both sizes: a layer-0 spike (embedding divergence), a stable interior plateau (<math id="A8.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha\in[0.7,2.2]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>0.7</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2.2</mn><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha\in[0.7,2.2]</annotation></semantics></math>), and an output-adjacent spike in the last 2–6 layers. The 3B model has a wider unstable tail (layers 30–35) but the entire interior stays bounded, and downstream quality (Table <a href="#A4.T6" title="Table 6 ‣ Appendix D Downstream Quality Benchmarks ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6</span></a>: 96–102% retained across MMLU/ARC/HellaSwag/WinoGrande) is unaffected because the lm_head and final norm are unshared and absorb the residual.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A8.F7" class="ltx_figure"><img src="2609.26147v1/x4.png" id="A8.F7.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" width="830" height="468" alt="Refer to caption">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A8.F7.8.4.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 7</span>: </span><span id="A8.F7.6.3" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Per-layer amplification factor <math id="A8.F7.4.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha(i)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha(i)</annotation></semantics></math> for Qwen2.5-1.5B (28 layers, blue) and Qwen2.5-3B (36 layers, red), normalized to relative depth <math id="A8.F7.5.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i/L" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i/L</annotation></semantics></math>. Both show interior <math id="A8.F7.6.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha&lt;2" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha&lt;2</annotation></semantics></math> (linear, not exponential, growth) plus boundary spikes at the embedding side and the lm_head side. The 3B model has a longer unstable tail but downstream benchmarks remain within 99% of the unshared baseline.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="A9" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix I </span>Version-over-Version Tightening: Three Family Pairs</h2>

<div id="A9.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A9.p1.1" class="ltx_p">§<a href="#S3" title="3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> reports a single version-over-version observation (Mistral-v0.1 vs. v0.3) and notes that establishing an industry trend would require additional family pairs. We complete this comparison here using publicly available 2023-vintage and 2024-vintage instruct variants. Table <a href="#A9.T10" title="Table 10 ‣ Appendix I Version-over-Version Tightening: Three Family Pairs ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">10</span></a> reports the mean per-block Frobenius ratio for three pairs.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A9.T10" class="ltx_table">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A9.T10.12.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 10</span>: </span><span id="A9.T10.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Per-block Frobenius perturbation across model generations within a family. Each row is the base<math id="A9.T10.2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math>aligned pair for that release (<span id="A9.T10.2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">-it</span> for Gemma, <span id="A9.T10.2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">-chat</span> for Llama-2 and Mistral-v0.1, <span id="A9.T10.2.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">-Instruct</span> otherwise). Two of three families (Gemma, Mistral) show order-of-magnitude tightening between 2023 and 2024 releases; Llama is essentially flat. The trend toward parameter-efficient post-training is real but <em id="A9.T10.2.1.4" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">not universal</em>.</span></figcaption>

</figure>
<div id="A9.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A9.p2.7" class="ltx_p">Two families (Gemma 1<math id="A9.p2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math>2 at <math id="A9.p2.2.m2" class="ltx_math_unparsed" alttext="12.4\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>12.4</mn><mo lspace="0.222em">×</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">12.4\times</annotation></semantics></math>, Mistral v0.1<math id="A9.p2.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math>v0.3 at <math id="A9.p2.4.m4" class="ltx_math_unparsed" alttext="23.3\times" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>23.3</mn><mo lspace="0.222em">×</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">23.3\times</annotation></semantics></math>) underwent dramatic tightening of post-training perturbation magnitude within one calendar year. Llama, by contrast, used comparable perturbation magnitude in the 2023 (Llama-2-7B-chat, <math id="A9.p2.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}=0.052" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0.052</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}=0.052</annotation></semantics></math>) and 2024 (Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, <math id="A9.p2.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}=0.045" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0.045</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}=0.045</annotation></semantics></math>) instruction-tuned releases. Possible explanations include (i) Llama’s training pipeline already used relatively conservative post-training in 2023, leaving little tightening headroom, or (ii) the 8B vs. 7B size step changed the per-parameter perturbation budget. We do not control for size or training procedure across these families and present the table as observational rather than causal evidence. Notably, the Llama-3.2 series (1B, 3B) reverts to much higher <math id="A9.p2.7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}</annotation></semantics></math> values (0.110–0.156, see Table <a href="#S3.T1" title="Table 1 ‣ 3.1 The Granularity Gap ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>), suggesting that intra-family variation can exceed the supposed inter-year tightening.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="A10" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix J </span>Per-Block <math id="A10.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>ϵ</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math> Profile for Gemma-2-2B</h2>

<div id="A10.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A10.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Gemma-2-2B is the lowest-sharing configuration in our study (12/26 blocks shared, 18% memory saving; Table <a href="#S4.T4" title="Table 4 ‣ 4 Application: LinkerLLM ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>). Figure <a href="#A10.F8" title="Figure 8 ‣ Appendix J Per-Block ϵ Profile for Gemma-2-2B ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">8</span></a> shows where its perturbation budget concentrates.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A10.F8" class="ltx_figure"><img src="2609.26147v1/x5.png" id="A10.F8.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" width="831" height="439" alt="Refer to caption">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A10.F8.18.9.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 8</span>: </span><span id="A10.F8.16.8" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Per-block Frobenius ratio <math id="A10.F8.9.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon(i)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon(i)</annotation></semantics></math> for Gemma-2-2B (base <math id="A10.F8.10.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math> instruct). Middle blocks (indices 5–14) carry the largest perturbation (<math id="A10.F8.11.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon\in[0.057,0.064]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>0.057</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0.064</mn><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon\in[0.057,0.064]</annotation></semantics></math>); late blocks (16–25) are progressively more preserved (<math id="A10.F8.12.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>ϵ</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math> drops to 0.022). At the tight <math id="A10.F8.13.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}=0.05" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.05</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}=0.05</annotation></semantics></math> used for Mistral/Qwen, 16/26 blocks fail; at the looser <math id="A10.F8.14.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}=0.07" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.07</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}=0.07</annotation></semantics></math> used in the Gemma deployment all blocks pass on <math id="A10.F8.15.7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>ϵ</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>, with the residual 14/26 failures driven by the dual-criterion cosine check (<math id="A10.F8.16.8.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{cos}}=0.999" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>cos</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.999</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{cos}}=0.999</annotation></semantics></math>) in fp16. The pattern (high perturbation concentrated in the middle, low perturbation in late layers) suggests that Gemma’s post-training updates are skewed away from the embedding-adjacent and lm-head-adjacent regions, unlike the more uniform profile of Mistral and Qwen.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="A11" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix K </span>Comparison with LoRA-Extraction and DeltaZip</h2>

<section id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">LoRA-extraction baseline.</h4>

<div id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.14" class="ltx_p">A natural alternative to block-level sharing is to compute <math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta W=W_{\text{variant}}-W_{\text{base}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>W</mi></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mtext>variant</mtext></msub><mo>−</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mtext>base</mtext></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta W=W_{\text{variant}}-W_{\text{base}}</annotation></semantics></math> per Linear sub-parameter, run a truncated SVD <math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta W\approx U_{r}\Sigma_{r}V_{r}^{\top}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>W</mi></mrow><mo>≈</mo><mrow><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Σ</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mi>r</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta W\approx U_{r}\Sigma_{r}V_{r}^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math>, and store only the rank-<math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>r</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r</annotation></semantics></math> factors. The LoRA-extraction storage at rank <math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>r</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r</annotation></semantics></math> is <math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r(d_{\text{in}}+d_{\text{out}})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>in</mtext></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>out</mtext></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r(d_{\text{in}}+d_{\text{out}})</annotation></semantics></math> parameters per Linear vs. <math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="d_{\text{in}}d_{\text{out}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>in</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>out</mtext></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">d_{\text{in}}d_{\text{out}}</annotation></semantics></math> for the full block; LoRA wins when <math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r&lt;d_{\text{in}}d_{\text{out}}/(d_{\text{in}}+d_{\text{out}})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>in</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>out</mtext></msub></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>in</mtext></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>out</mtext></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r&lt;d_{\text{in}}d_{\text{out}}/(d_{\text{in}}+d_{\text{out}})</annotation></semantics></math>, i.e., <math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.8.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r&lt;d/2" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r&lt;d/2</annotation></semantics></math> for square matrices. We measure the smallest rank <math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.9.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>r</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r</annotation></semantics></math> such that the Frobenius reconstruction error <math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.10.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\|\Delta W-U_{r}\Sigma_{r}V_{r}^{\top}\|_{F}/\|\Delta W\|_{F}\leq 0.05" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>W</mi></mrow><mo>−</mo><mrow><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Σ</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mi>r</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mi>F</mi></msub><mo>/</mo><msub><mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>W</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mi>F</mi></msub></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mn>0.05</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\|\Delta W-U_{r}\Sigma_{r}V_{r}^{\top}\|_{F}/\|\Delta W\|_{F}\leq 0.05</annotation></semantics></math> (95% reconstruction) and report the resulting per-block ratio (LoRA params / full params); Table <a href="#A11.T11" title="Table 11 ‣ LoRA-extraction baseline. ‣ Appendix K Comparison with LoRA-Extraction and DeltaZip ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">11</span></a> summarizes three Qwen2.5 sizes spanning the post-training band. Across all three model sizes and across all but one of the 88 measured blocks, <math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.11.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta W" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>W</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta W</annotation></semantics></math> has a sufficiently broad SVD spectrum that the rank-<math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.12.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>r</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r</annotation></semantics></math> approximation costs <em id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.14.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">more</em> bytes than the original block (<math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.13.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\text{ratio}&gt;1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mtext>ratio</mtext><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\text{ratio}&gt;1</annotation></semantics></math>). LoRA-extraction is therefore not a competitive memory baseline for full-weight post-trained variants at high Frobenius fidelity; Linker aliasing stores zero extra bytes by pointer-swapping the donor block. This is consistent with the known result that full fine-tuning <math id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.14.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta W" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>W</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta W</annotation></semantics></math> is empirically full-rank in numerical terms <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(Hu et al., <a href="#bib.bib6" title="" class="ltx_ref">2022</a>)</cite>: low-rank <em id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.14.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">performance</em> is preserved because much of the spectrum is irrelevant for downstream loss, but low-rank <em id="A11.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.14.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">Frobenius</em> fidelity is not, so a LoRA-extraction baseline at fixed Frobenius tolerance cannot compress.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A11.T11" class="ltx_table">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A11.T11.6.3.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 11</span>: </span><span id="A11.T11.4.2" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">LoRA-extraction baseline: per-block ratio of rank-truncated LoRA storage to full block storage at Frobenius reconstruction tolerance tol. Ratio <math id="A11.T11.3.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\geq 1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi></mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\geq 1</annotation></semantics></math> means LoRA-extraction is worse than storing the original block. Linker aliasing stores 0 extra bytes (pointer alias to the donor). Block-level aliasing therefore Pareto-dominates LoRA-extraction on these post-trained variants, whose <math id="A11.T11.4.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta W" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>W</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta W</annotation></semantics></math> spectra are too broad for low-rank approximation at Frobenius fidelity.</span></figcaption>

<section id="A12" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix L </span>Loss-Along-Interpolation: Full Per-Family Data</h2>

<div id="A12.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A12.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Section <a href="#S3.SS4" title="3.4 Loss-Along-Interpolation: Block-Level Path Test ‣ 3 Results ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.4</span></a> reports the cross-family loss-along-interpolation summary. This appendix gives the full per-family table and the boundary-family + continued-pretrained figures; the per-block traces are in the released JSONs.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A12.T13" class="ltx_table">

Per-block max <math id="A12.T13.6.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta L" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta L</annotation></semantics></math> is the worst per-block barrier; full-model peak <math id="A12.T13.7.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta L" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta L</annotation></semantics></math> is the maximum of <math id="A12.T13.8.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="L_{\text{full}}(\alpha)-L_{\text{variant}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>L</mi><mtext>full</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>α</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>−</mo><msub><mi>L</mi><mtext>variant</mtext></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">L_{\text{full}}(\alpha)-L_{\text{variant}}</annotation></semantics></math> over <math id="A12.T13.9.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha\in[0,1]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha\in[0,1]</annotation></semantics></math>. All values in nats; <math id="A12.T13.10.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="L" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>L</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">L</annotation></semantics></math> is the number of transformer blocks. Post-trained families have negligible barriers; the boundary family Llama-3.2-1B has small per-block barriers concentrated at the boundary blocks but the full-model path is also broken. Continued-pretrained variants (Qwen-Coder, Qwen-Math) have dramatically larger barriers across both metrics.</span></figcaption>

The full-model interpolation curve (orange) rises from <math id="A12.F9.12.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="3.22" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>3.22</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">3.22</annotation></semantics></math> nats at the variant (<math id="A12.F9.13.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha{=}0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha{=}0</annotation></semantics></math>) to <math id="A12.F9.14.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="5.91" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>5.91</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">5.91</annotation></semantics></math> nats at the base (<math id="A12.F9.15.7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha{=}1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha{=}1</annotation></semantics></math>). The deployed-shared set on this family (Table <a href="#S4.T4" title="Table 4 ‣ 4 Application: LinkerLLM ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>: 13/16 blocks at <math id="A12.F9.16.8.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}{=}0.20" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.20</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}{=}0.20</annotation></semantics></math>) excludes exactly the two boundary-block positions identified here.</span></figcaption>

<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A12.F10.16.8.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 10</span>: </span><span id="A12.F10.14.7" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Loss-along-interpolation on Qwen2.5-1.5B base <math id="A12.F10.8.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math> Qwen2.5-Coder-1.5B (continued pretraining, <math id="A12.F10.9.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}\approx 0.36" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>≈</mo><mn>0.36</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}\approx 0.36</annotation></semantics></math>, cos <math id="A12.F10.10.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\sim 0.70" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi></mi><mo>∼</mo><mn>0.70</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sim 0.70</annotation></semantics></math>). Per-block paths develop barriers of <math id="A12.F10.11.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0.5" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0.5</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0.5</annotation></semantics></math>–<math id="A12.F10.12.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="9.0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>9.0</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">9.0</annotation></semantics></math> nats; 27 of 28 blocks have per-block max <math id="A12.F10.13.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta L&gt;0.1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0.1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta L&gt;0.1</annotation></semantics></math> nats. The full-model interpolation rises by <math id="A12.F10.14.7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="6.7" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>6.7</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">6.7</annotation></semantics></math> nats. Continued pretraining is therefore not a small extension of post-training in either parameter geometry or loss landscape.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<figure id="A12.F11" class="ltx_figure"><img src="2609.26147v1/x8.png" id="A12.F11.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" width="790" height="565" alt="Refer to caption">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="A12.F11.16.8.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 11</span>: </span><span id="A12.F11.14.7" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Loss-along-interpolation on Qwen2.5-1.5B base <math id="A12.F11.8.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math> Qwen2.5-Math-1.5B (continued pretraining, <math id="A12.F11.9.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{\epsilon}\approx 0.47" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>≈</mo><mn>0.47</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{\epsilon}\approx 0.47</annotation></semantics></math>, cos <math id="A12.F11.10.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\sim 0.53" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi></mi><mo>∼</mo><mn>0.53</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sim 0.53</annotation></semantics></math>). Block 0 has a per-block barrier of <math id="A12.F11.11.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\sim" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>∼</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\sim</annotation></semantics></math>6.5 nats; 10 of 28 blocks have per-block max above <math id="A12.F11.12.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0.1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0.1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0.1</annotation></semantics></math> nats. The full-model interpolation rises by <math id="A12.F11.13.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="7.8" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>7.8</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">7.8</annotation></semantics></math> nats. The Coder and Math continued-pretrained branches both show the same loss-barrier signature, confirming that the continued-pretraining <math id="A12.F11.14.7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\to" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math> outside-the-aligned-neighborhood pattern is not idiosyncratic to a single specialization.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<section id="A12.SS0.SSS0.Px1" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Deployment guideline.</h4>

<div id="A12.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A12.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.2" class="ltx_p">The geometric criterion (cos, <math id="A12.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>ϵ</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>) flags block 0 and block 15 as the highest-<math id="A12.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>ϵ</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math> blocks of Llama-3.2-1B, and §<a href="#S4" title="4 Application: LinkerLLM ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>’s threshold sweep excludes them from sharing automatically. The loss-interpolation protocol confirms the geometric criterion’s exclusion decisions correspond to real loss-landscape barriers. For continued-pretrained derivatives the same diagnosis applies, but the entire variant fails the loss-interpolation test and no useful share-set exists; Linker degenerates gracefully to Independent (Section <a href="#S4" title="4 Application: LinkerLLM ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>, Appendix <a href="#A2" title="Appendix B Lazy Loader: Algorithm and Architecture ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">B</span></a>).</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>

<td id="A14.T15.4.3.2.6" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_border_bb" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><span id="A14.T15.4.3.2.6.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">56.3s</span></td>
<td id="A14.T15.4.3.2.7" class="ltx_td ltx_nopad_r ltx_align_center ltx_border_bb" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><span id="A14.T15.4.3.2.7.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">11.9s</span></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="A15" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix O </span>Generation Quality Under Aggressive Sharing</h2>

<div id="A15.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A15.p1.2" class="ltx_p">As a supplementary probe (not the primary quality evidence; see Table 5 in the main text for benchmark-based evaluation), we measure free-form generation overlap when <em id="A15.p1.2.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">all</em> transformer blocks are replaced with the base model’s blocks (worst-case, <math id="A15.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{frob}}=\infty" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>frob</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{frob}}=\infty</annotation></semantics></math>). On 12 diverse prompts, Qwen2.5-3B achieves BLEU 0.50 / ROUGE-L 0.42 and Mistral-7B achieves BLEU 0.44 / ROUGE-L 0.23 between original and shared outputs. Both families produce factually correct, coherent outputs; the lower ROUGE-L for Mistral reflects a style shift (base model generates in continuation style rather than instruction-following format). The 0% token-level exact match is expected: even tiny weight differences (<math id="A15.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon\sim 0.01" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>∼</mo><mn>0.01</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon\sim 0.01</annotation></semantics></math>) cause autoregressive divergence after a few tokens. We note that BLEU and ROUGE-L on 12 prompts constitute a coarse probe; comprehensive generation evaluation (MT-Bench, AlpacaEval) is needed for deployment decisions but is beyond the scope of this weight-space analysis.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="A16" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix P </span>Per-Sub-Parameter Cosine Breakdown for Gemma-2-2B</h2>

<div id="A16.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A16.p1.7" class="ltx_p">Section <a href="#S5" title="5 Discussion and Conclusion ‣ Block-Level Weight-Space Structure Persists Under Post-Training: An Empirical Study Across LLM Families" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a> notes that Gemma’s deployed share set <math id="A16.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{0\!\dots\!11\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mpadded style="width:0.330em;" width="0.330em"><mn>0</mn></mpadded><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mpadded style="width:0.580em;" width="0.580em"><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi></mpadded><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mn>11</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{0\!\dots\!11\}</annotation></semantics></math> is decided by the mean of per-sub-parameter cosines. A natural concern is that the rejected blocks <math id="A16.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{12\!\dots\!25\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mpadded style="width:0.830em;" width="0.830em"><mn>12</mn></mpadded><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mpadded style="width:0.580em;" width="0.580em"><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi></mpadded><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mn>25</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{12\!\dots\!25\}</annotation></semantics></math> might be driven below the <math id="A16.p1.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau_{\text{cos}}{=}0.999" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>τ</mi><mtext>cos</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.999</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau_{\text{cos}}{=}0.999</annotation></semantics></math> threshold by one or two outlier sub-parameters (e.g., <span id="A16.p1.7.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">q_proj</span> or <span id="A16.p1.7.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">down_proj</span>), in which case a max- or min-cosine criterion would reclassify the share set. We measure this directly by computing, for every block <math id="A16.p1.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i\in\{0,\ldots,25\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>25</mn><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i\in\{0,\ldots,25\}</annotation></semantics></math>, the cosine <math id="A16.p1.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\cos(\theta_{i}^{(s)},\theta_{i}^{\prime(s)})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msubsup><mi>θ</mi><mi>i</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>s</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>θ</mi><mi>i</mi><mrow><mo mathsize="1.420em">′</mo><mo lspace="0em">⁣</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>s</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></msubsup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\cos(\theta_{i}^{(s)},\theta_{i}^{\prime(s)})</annotation></semantics></math> for each of the 11 sub-parameters <math id="A16.p1.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="s" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>s</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">s</annotation></semantics></math> (4 attention projections, 3 MLP projections, 4 layernorms), then reporting min/mean/max over <math id="A16.p1.7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="s" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>s</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">s</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A16.T16" class="ltx_table">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A16.T16.9.4.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 16</span>: </span><span id="A16.T16.6.3" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Per-sub-parameter cosine statistics for Gemma-2-2B blocks, averaged within group (<math id="A16.T16.4.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n{=}12" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n{=}12</annotation></semantics></math> shared, <math id="A16.T16.5.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n{=}14" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>14</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n{=}14</annotation></semantics></math> rejected). “Gap” is mean<math id="A16.T16.6.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="-" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>−</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-</annotation></semantics></math>min, a measure of how much one outlier sub-parameter could pull the block mean down. The deployed-shared and rejected groups have nearly identical gaps, so the rejection of blocks 12–25 is <em id="A16.T16.6.3.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">not</em> driven by sub-parameter outliers; the entire cosine distribution shifts uniformly with block depth.</span></figcaption>
<table id="A16.T16.10" class="ltx_tabular ltx_centering ltx_guessed_headers ltx_align_middle">

<td id="A16.T16.10.2.1.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_border_t" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><span id="A16.T16.10.2.1.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">0.99844</span></td>
<td id="A16.T16.10.2.1.3" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_border_t" style="padding-left:4.0pt;padding-right:4.0pt;"><span id="A16.T16.10.2.1.3.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">0.99929</span></td>
