<div id="A16.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A16.p2.2" class="ltx_p">The minimum-sub-parameter cosine in any rejected block is 0.99741 (block 24, <span id="A16.p2.2.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">self_attn.k_proj</span>); the minimum in any shared block is 0.99780 (block 11, <span id="A16.p2.2.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">mlp.down_proj</span>). A max-cosine criterion (1.0 on every block, since all blocks have at least one sub-parameter at <math id="A16.p2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0.99999" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0.99999</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0.99999</annotation></semantics></math>+) would share <em id="A16.p2.2.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">all</em> 26 blocks and lose the deployed Gemma quality differentiation. A min-cosine criterion at <math id="A16.p2.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tau{=}0.998" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>τ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.998</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau{=}0.998</annotation></semantics></math> would share 14/26 blocks rather than 12/26 (two extra early blocks), while still preserving the same depth-dependent share boundary. The mean-of-cosines criterion used in <span id="A16.p2.2.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">aliasing.py</span> is therefore neither overly aggressive nor unduly conservative; it captures the dominant depth signal robustly.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="A17" class="ltx_appendix">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_appendix">
<span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix Q </span>Gradient-Locality Probe</h2>

<div id="A17.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A17.p1.2" class="ltx_p">A natural mechanistic hypothesis for block-level persistence is <em id="A17.p1.2.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">gradient locality</em>: blocks that receive small gradient signal during fine-tuning are exactly the ones that remain similar to the base checkpoint after post-training. We test the simple gradient-magnitude version of this hypothesis on Gemma-2-2B as a controlled falsification target: if it holds, blocks <math id="A17.p1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{0\!\dots\!11\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mpadded style="width:0.330em;" width="0.330em"><mn>0</mn></mpadded><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mpadded style="width:0.580em;" width="0.580em"><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi></mpadded><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mn>11</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{0\!\dots\!11\}</annotation></semantics></math> (deployed-shared) should have systematically smaller gradient norms than blocks <math id="A17.p1.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{12\!\dots\!25\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mpadded style="width:0.830em;" width="0.830em"><mn>12</mn></mpadded><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mpadded style="width:0.580em;" width="0.580em"><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi></mpadded><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mn>25</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{12\!\dots\!25\}</annotation></semantics></math> (rejected) on a representative LM loss.</p>
</div>
<div id="A17.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A17.p2.6" class="ltx_p"><span id="A17.p2.6.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Setup.</span> We load the base <span id="A17.p2.6.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">gemma-2-2b</span> checkpoint in bf16 on a single RTX 3090, compute <math id="A17.p2.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{\text{LM}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mo>∇</mo><mi>θ</mi></msub><msub><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">ℒ</mi><mtext>LM</mtext></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{\text{LM}}</annotation></semantics></math> on 12 generic English calibration strings (factual, code, narrative, QA-format), and accumulate per-example gradients to obtain a smoothed estimate. For each block <math id="A17.p2.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> we report the relative gradient norm <math id="A17.p2.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\|g_{i}\|_{2}/\|\theta_{i}\|_{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mi>g</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><msub><mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mi>θ</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\|g_{i}\|_{2}/\|\theta_{i}\|_{2}</annotation></semantics></math> (gradient norm normalized by parameter norm, which controls for the natural growth in <math id="A17.p2.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\|\theta_{i}\|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mi>θ</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\|\theta_{i}\|</annotation></semantics></math> with depth). We then correlate <math id="A17.p2.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\|g_{i}\|/\|\theta_{i}\|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mi>g</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mi>θ</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\|g_{i}\|/\|\theta_{i}\|</annotation></semantics></math> with the empirically measured <math id="A17.p2.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1-\overline{\cos}_{i})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>cos</mi><mo stretchy="true">¯</mo></mover><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1-\overline{\cos}_{i})</annotation></semantics></math> across the 26 blocks.</p>
</div>
<div id="A17.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A17.p3.9" class="ltx_p"><span id="A17.p3.9.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Result.</span> Pearson <math id="A17.p3.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r=-0.120" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>0.120</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r=-0.120</annotation></semantics></math> and Spearman <math id="A17.p3.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\rho=-0.104" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ρ</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>0.104</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\rho=-0.104</annotation></semantics></math> between <math id="A17.p3.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\|g_{i}\|/\|\theta_{i}\|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mi>g</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mi>θ</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\|g_{i}\|/\|\theta_{i}\|</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="A17.p3.4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1{-}\overline{\cos}_{i})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>cos</mi><mo stretchy="true">¯</mo></mover><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1{-}\overline{\cos}_{i})</annotation></semantics></math>, essentially zero and slightly opposite in sign to the prediction of naive gradient locality. At the group level, shared blocks exhibit <em id="A17.p3.9.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">higher</em> average relative gradient (<math id="A17.p3.5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0.0447" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0.0447</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0.0447</annotation></semantics></math>) than rejected blocks (<math id="A17.p3.6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0.0402" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0.0402</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0.0402</annotation></semantics></math>), while shared blocks have <em id="A17.p3.9.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">lower</em> average <math id="A17.p3.7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1{-}\cos)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mi>cos</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1{-}\cos)</annotation></semantics></math> (<math id="A17.p3.8.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="7.1{\times}10^{-4}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>7.1</mn><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">7.1{\times}10^{-4}</annotation></semantics></math>) than rejected blocks (<math id="A17.p3.9.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1.13{\times}10^{-3}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1.13</mn><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1.13{\times}10^{-3}</annotation></semantics></math>). The two metrics yield inconsistent conclusions.</p>
</div>
<figure id="A17.T17" class="ltx_table">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A17.T17.6.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 17</span>: </span><span id="A17.T17.7.2" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Gradient-locality probe: average values within deployed-shared vs. rejected groups for Gemma-2-2B. Higher gradient does not imply higher empirical divergence; the relationship is in fact slightly anti-correlated.</span></figcaption>
<table id="A17.T17.4" class="ltx_tabular ltx_centering ltx_guessed_headers ltx_align_middle">
<thead class="ltx_thead">
<tr id="A17.T17.2.2" class="ltx_tr">
<th id="A17.T17.2.2.3" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_th ltx_th_column ltx_th_row ltx_border_tt"><span id="A17.T17.2.2.3.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Group</span></th>
<th id="A17.T17.1.1.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_th ltx_th_column ltx_border_tt"><math id="A17.T17.1.1.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\|g\|/\|\theta\|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo maxsize="0.900em" minsize="0.900em" stretchy="true">‖</mo><mi mathsize="0.900em">g</mi><mo maxsize="0.900em" minsize="0.900em" stretchy="true">‖</mo></mrow><mo maxsize="0.900em" minsize="0.900em" stretchy="true" symmetric="true">/</mo><mrow><mo maxsize="0.900em" minsize="0.900em" stretchy="true">‖</mo><mi mathsize="0.900em">θ</mi><mo maxsize="0.900em" minsize="0.900em" stretchy="true">‖</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\|g\|/\|\theta\|</annotation></semantics></math></th>
<th id="A17.T17.2.2.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_th ltx_th_column ltx_border_tt"><math id="A17.T17.2.2.2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1-\cos" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn mathsize="0.900em">1</mn><mo mathsize="0.900em">−</mo><mi mathsize="0.900em">cos</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1-\cos</annotation></semantics></math></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody class="ltx_tbody">
<tr id="A17.T17.3.3" class="ltx_tr">
<th id="A17.T17.3.3.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_th ltx_th_row ltx_border_t"><span id="A17.T17.3.3.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Shared (0–11)</span></th>
<td id="A17.T17.3.3.3" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_border_t"><span id="A17.T17.3.3.3.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">0.0447</span></td>
<td id="A17.T17.3.3.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_border_t"><math id="A17.T17.3.3.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="7.1{\times}10^{-4}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn mathsize="0.900em">7.1</mn><mo lspace="0.222em" mathsize="0.900em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msup><mn mathsize="0.900em">10</mn><mrow><mo mathsize="0.900em">−</mo><mn mathsize="0.900em">4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">7.1{\times}10^{-4}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A17.T17.4.4" class="ltx_tr">
<th id="A17.T17.4.4.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_th ltx_th_row ltx_border_bb"><span id="A17.T17.4.4.2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Rejected (12–25)</span></th>
<td id="A17.T17.4.4.3" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_border_bb"><span id="A17.T17.4.4.3.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">0.0402</span></td>
<td id="A17.T17.4.4.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_border_bb"><math id="A17.T17.4.4.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1.13{\times}10^{-3}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn mathsize="0.900em">1.13</mn><mo lspace="0.222em" mathsize="0.900em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msup><mn mathsize="0.900em">10</mn><mrow><mo mathsize="0.900em">−</mo><mn mathsize="0.900em">3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1.13{\times}10^{-3}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</figure>
<div id="A17.p4" class="ltx_para">
<p id="A17.p4.3" class="ltx_p"><span id="A17.p4.3.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Interpretation.</span> The dominant axis along which <math id="A17.p4.1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1{-}\cos)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mi>cos</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1{-}\cos)</annotation></semantics></math> varies is depth: it rises near-monotonically with layer index <math id="A17.p4.2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>. The gradient-norm profile, by contrast, peaks in the middle layers (12–17) and decays at deep layers (22–25). Because the two profiles do not co-vary, the simple “high gradient <math id="A17.p4.3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Rightarrow" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo stretchy="false">⇒</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Rightarrow</annotation></semantics></math> large post-training perturbation” hypothesis fails to predict the cosine-criterion share set. This rules out one mechanistic hypothesis but leaves several open: post-training updates may be <em id="A17.p4.3.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">cumulative</em> along depth (each layer absorbs a small change but compounds the perturbation passing through it); they may be <em id="A17.p4.3.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">directional</em> in a way that gradient <em id="A17.p4.3.4" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">magnitude</em> does not capture (e.g., the gradient may be small at shared blocks but consistently aligned across SFT steps at rejected blocks); or the gemma-2-2b-it instruct training may have used SFT data with gradient locality patterns very different from the generic LM calibration set we used. Disambiguating these would require the actual SFT checkpoints or training-data distribution, neither of which is publicly available for gemma-2-2b-it. We therefore report this as a <em id="A17.p4.3.5" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">negative</em> result: the simplest gradient-magnitude version of gradient locality is falsified for Gemma’s deployed share set, and a refined mechanistic explanation remains open.</p>
</div>
</section>
</article>
